What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a hard time after I eat. I don’t really like saying it, but I’ve been making myself throw up sometimes… like in the bathroom at work, or as soon as I get home. It’s embarrassing, and I know it’s not good. It just… it’s started feeling like the only way I can calm myself down, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often limit the amount or type of food you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t try to starve myself or anything, but on days where I feel like I ate too much, I’ll kind of make up for it. Like I might not eat until really late the next day, or I’ll make myself walk more, exercise some… even if my legs are already hurting from standing at the cleaners all day. I know that probably doesn’t sound great.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I… don’t really know. I try not to check the scale too much, honestly. My clothes don’t feel wildly different or anything, so I wouldn’t say it’s been a big amount… at least I don’t think so. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: It’s… more often than I want to admit. Sometimes after I eat, especially if I feel like I had too much, I end up going to the bathroom and making myself throw up. It’s happened at work, and sometimes right when I get home. I don’t do it because I want to be difficult or anything… it just feels like the only thing that calms me down in that moment. Is that… the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How often do you limit the amount or type of food you eat?\n\nPatient: I guess… pretty often, but I don’t think I’m trying to be extreme about it. It’s more like if I feel like I ate too much the day before, I’ll try to make up for it by not eating until late, or I’ll walk more or exercise some… even when my legs are already hurting from standing at the cleaners all day. I know that’s probably not the best answer.\n\nDoctor: How often do you eat an amount of food that you consider to be much more than most people would eat in a similar time period?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, that does happen. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but sometimes it’s like I start and then I don’t really stop. Like I can get through a whole bag of chips, some pastries from the corner store, leftovers, and sweet drinks all in one sitting… and then I kind of look up and realize how much is gone. Is that what you mean by more than most people would eat?\n\nDoctor: How often do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time, like what you described with the bag of chips and pastries?\n\nPatient: I guess… if I’m being honest, it’s been going on for months now. Most weeks it happens at least a couple times, where I eat way more than I meant to in a short amount of time. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah. And then I usually feel like I have to make up for it somehow, like throwing up or not eating for a while, or trying to exercise it off. Is that the kind of answer you need?\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s hard to say exactly. I don’t do it every single time I eat, but… it’s been happening a lot. Most weeks, probably several times. Sometimes I’ll go to the bathroom at work after eating, or I’ll wait until I get home, and I make myself throw up because I feel so panicked. I know that sounds bad… I just hate admitting it. It feels like the only thing that calms me down sometimes.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you eat a large amount of food in a short time, like with the bag of chips and pastries you mentioned earlier?\n\nPatient: Um… most weeks it’s at least a couple times. I don’t want to exaggerate, but for months now there are usually two or more times where I eat way more than I meant to in a short period. And then I feel like I need to… make up for it after, like throwing up or not eating, or trying to exercise it off. Is that specific enough?\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Not every single time… I mean, I wish I could say it was rare, but it isn’t. A lot of the time after I eat like that, I get so panicked that I end up making myself throw up. Sometimes it’s even in the bathroom at work, or right when I get home. I hate admitting that, but it’s started to feel like the only way I can calm down. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up every time you eat a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Not every time, no… but a lot of the time I do. I keep making myself throw up after I eat too much, sometimes even in the bathroom at work or right when I get home. I’m embarrassed to say it, but when I get that panicked feeling, it’s started to feel like the only thing that calms me down. Is that okay to say?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
